The user wants a complete interactive 3D aquarium using Three.js based on a detailed prompt. I should create a single HTML file containing all the code: fish with animation, AI behavior, environment (glass tank, sand, rocks, seaweed), bubbles, feeding system, lighting, OrbitControls, UI panels, buttons.

Let me write a comprehensive but manageable HTML file. Use CDN r128 and OrbitControls.

Key elements:
- Tank dimensions: 36x24x20 (width x height? Actually probably x=36, y=24, z=20). Let's use W=36, H=24, D=20. Fish swim inside.
- Fish creation function: body (scaled sphere), eyes, animated tail, fins, 8 color schemes, random scale 0.6–1.2.
- Behavior: separation, wall reflection, random wandering, food pursuit, growth.
- Bubbles: 30, rising while swaying, reset.
- Feeding: click → raycast to plane or tank; food falls with gravity; fish within radius 15 pursue; eating → remove food + 5% growth; food removed when it reaches the bottom.
- Lighting: ambient, shadowed directional (2048, PCFSoft), 2 point lights (blue/cyan); light toggle button.
- OrbitControls: min 10, max 60, maxPolarAngle PI/1.8, damping.
- UI: info panel top-left (title gradient, controls, buttons), stats top-right (fish count, FPS), buttons: add fish, more bubbles, light toggle.
- FogExp2, background gradient (scene background color or CSS? Use scene background color; gradient via fog).
- Glass UI CSS.

Write clean code. Let's produce the final answer with the complete HTML file and brief usage notes.

Details to watch out for:

Tank glass: BoxGeometry(36,24,20), MeshPhysicalMaterial transmission 0.95, transparent, opacity... In r128 transmission works with physicallyCorrectLights? Transmission works in r128. Also wireframe edges: EdgesGeometry + LineSegments.

Sand floor: PlaneGeometry(36,20,32,24) rotated by -PI/2, positioned at y=0 (tank bottom is y=0, center y=12). Displace vertices randomly in z direction. receiveShadow.

Rocks: 8 DodecahedronGeometry(random 0.8–1.6), displace vertices, MeshStandardMaterial gray, cast/receive shadows, placed on the floor.

Seaweed: 12 clumps, each 2–4 blades: CatmullRomCurve3 with points swaying upward from the base, TubeGeometry(curve, 20, 0.15, 5), green material, sway animation by rotating the group slightly over time.

Bubbles: SphereGeometry(0.15–0.4), MeshPhysicalMaterial transparent, low opacity, roughness 0.1, metalness 0. Rising: y += speed*dt, x/z sway via sin(time+phase). When y > H-1, reset to a random position near the bottom.

Food: small sphere (0.3), orange/brown. Falls: vy -= g*dt, y decreases; stop when y < 0.3 (bounces slightly or just rests). Fish check distance < 15 → set targetFood. When fish is close to food (<1.5): remove food, grow fish by 1.5x, maybe spawn a few extra bubbles.

Fish movement:
- velocity vector; speed ~ 2–4 units/sec * individual speed factor.
- Separation: for each pair within avoidanceRadius (e.g., 3), push apart.
- Walls: if x > W/2 - margin, steer velocity.x negative (lerp). Same for others. Vertical bounds: keep y between 1.5 and H-2.
- Random wandering: every 2–5 seconds (random timer per fish), add a random direction component.
- Orientation: rotate fish group toward the velocity direction: compute angle = Math.atan2(vx, vz)? Fish model faces +Z? Build the fish facing +X? Simpler: build the fish along the Z axis (head at +Z). Then yaw = Math.atan2(velocity.x, velocity.z). Smoothly lerp the current yaw toward the target (angle wrap handling). Also slight pitch based on vy.

Tail animation: tail.rotation.y = sin(time*tailSpeed + phase)*0.5 — actually tail swings laterally, so rotation around Y axis (if fish faces +Z). The prompt says Z-axis rotation, but that depends on orientation. I'll do lateral swing with rotation.y. Fins: small rotation oscillation.

Fish group structure:
- body: SphereGeometry(1,16,12) scaled (0.35, 0.45, 1.2)? Let's make the body length about 2 units. SphereGeometry(1, 16, 12).scale(0.32, 0.5, 1). Head at +Z.
- Eyes: 2 spheres of radius 0.12, white, positioned at (±0.18, 0.1, 0.75); pupils are small black spheres of radius 0.06 slightly forward.
- Tail: cone or flattened sphere: SphereGeometry(1, 12, 8).scale(0.06, 0.5, 0.7), positioned at z = -1.3, pivot: put the tail mesh inside a group "tailPivot" at z=-1.1, offset the mesh by z=-0.25. Animate tailPivot.rotation.y.
- Dorsal fin: flattened sphere or custom shape: SphereGeometry(1,10,8).scale(0.05, 0.35, 0.5) at (0, 0.42, 0).
- Pectoral fins: 2 flattened spheres scale(0.05,0.25,0.3) at (±0.3, -0.05, 0.3), animate slight rotation.

Color scheme array: 8 entries with {body, belly?, fin}. Keep simple: body color hex, fin color slightly darker/lighter. Use MeshStandardMaterial.

Size: overall fish scale 0.6–1.2 → group.scale.setScalar(s). Body length about 2*1.2 = 2.4 units. Good.

FPS counter: frame count, update every 500ms.

Click handling: distinguish drag vs click (record mousedown position, on mouseup if movement < 5px → feed). Raycast onto an invisible plane? Better: raycast against the tank glass box; if hit, place food slightly inside at that point. Or intersect with a horizontal plane at some depth. Simplest and most robust: raycaster.ray.intersectPlane (a vertical plane facing the camera? no). Just use the glass mesh intersection; if none, use a plane perpendicular to the camera passing through the tank center: plane normal = camera direction, constant = center. That works well. Clamp the food position inside the tank with a margin.

Also spawn a few bubbles at the food location as feedback.

- AmbientLight(0x404040, 0.4)
- DirectionalLight(0xfff2d0, 1.0), position (20, 35, 15), castShadow, shadow.mapSize 2048, shadow camera range ±30.

- Light toggle: directional.intensity = 1.0 : 0.15.

Renderer: antialias, shadowMap enabled, PCFSoftShadowMap. Scene fog: FogExp2(0x0a2a4a, 0.012). Background: 0x06182e or similar.

- dt = clock.getDelta(), clamp at 0.05.

Fish update pseudocode:
```
for f of fishArray:
  // Food pursuit
  if f.targetFood && food still exists:
     dir = food.pos - f.pos; normalize
     f.velocity.lerp(dir * f.speed*1.4, 0.05)
     if dist < 1.6: eat
  else:
     f.targetFood = null
     // Look for food within 15
     for food: if dist < 15: target = food; break
  // Separation
  for other: d = pos.distanceTo(other.pos); if d < f.avoidanceRadius: push away
  // Wall steering
  margin 2
  if pos.x > W/2-m: vel.x -= ...
  Actually: steer: desired change: vel.x *= dampen; add force: vel.x -= (pos.x - (W/2-m)) * k
  // Random wander
  f.wanderTimer -= dt; if <=0: add random vector * 0.5; reset timer to 2+rand*3
  // Speed limit
  v = f.velocity; len = v.length(); target = f.speed; if len > 0: v.normalize().multiplyScalar(THREE.MathUtils.lerp(len, target, 0.05))
  // Integrate
  pos.addScaledVector(v, dt)
  // Hard clamp
  // Orientation
  yawTarget = atan2(v.x, v.z)
  smooth: f.yaw += angleDiff(f.yaw, yawTarget) * 0.08
  f.mesh.rotation.y = f.yaw
  pitch: f.pitch = lerp(f.pitch, -atan2(v.y, horizontalLen)*0.5, 0.05); mesh.rotation.x = f.pitch
  // Animation
  t = elapsed
  tailPivot.rotation.y = sin(t*f.tailSpeed + f.phase) * 0.55
  leftFin.rotation.z = 0.3 + sin(t*3+f.phase)*0.15 etc.
```

Angle diff helper: ((target - current + PI*3) % (2PI)) - PI.

Eating: fish grows: f.grow = f.mesh.scale * 1.05 (track baseScale). Remove food from foodArray and scene.

Add fish button: createFish(random position).

- #info { top: 16px; left: 16px; }

```
const camDir = new THREE.Vector3();
camera.getWorldDirection(camDir);
plane.normal.copy(camDir);
plane.constant = -camDir.dot(new THREE.Vector3(0, H/2, 0)); // plane passing through center
raycaster.ray.intersectPlane(plane, point)
```
Then clamp x to [-W/2+1, W/2-1], y to [1, H-1], z to [-D/2+1, D/2-1]. Food starts falling from that point. Good — also avoids conflict with OrbitControls clicks: use pointerdown/up with a movement threshold.

Food physics: vy -= 9.8*dt... underwater: slower fall, use g=6, terminal velocity ~2.5. Implementation: f.vy = max(f.vy - 6*dt, -2.5); y += vy*dt. When y <= 0.4: y = 0.4; vy = 0; mark as settled (maybe remove after 10 seconds? Prompt says remove when it reaches the bottom — hmm "удаление корма при достижении дна" — remove food when it reaches the bottom? Seems odd but okay: maybe means when it settles on the bottom, fish eat it there. Let's have it rest at the bottom and let fish eat it there. Actually, to be safe, follow the prompt: fish can eat it anywhere; if it stays on the bottom for more than 15 seconds, remove it.)

Bubbles: store {mesh, speed, phase, baseX, baseZ}. Update: y += speed*dt; x = baseX + sin(elapsed*1.5 + phase)*0.4; z = baseZ + cos(elapsed*1.2 + phase)*0.4; if y > H-0.5: reset y to 0.3, new random baseX/baseZ.

Seaweed sway: for each clump: clump.rotation.x = sin(t*0.8 + i)*0.06; clump.rotation.z = cos(t*0.6+i)*0.06.

Rock displacement: iterate position attribute, multiply by (1 + (Math.random()-0.5)*0.35).

Sand displacement: iterate vertices, z += random*0.25 (before rotation). After rotateX(-PI/2), local z becomes world y. Set material color 0xd9c08a, roughness 1.

Edges: new THREE.LineSegments(new THREE.EdgesGeometry(boxGeo), new THREE.LineBasicMaterial({color:0x88ccff, transparent:true, opacity:0.5})).

Glass material: MeshPhysicalMaterial({color:0xbfe8ff, transmission:0.95, opacity:0.15, transparent:true, roughness:0.05, metalness:0, side:THREE.DoubleSide? BackSide might be better to avoid front-face occlusion — but raycast isn't needed. Use DoubleSide with low opacity}). depthWrite false to avoid artifacts.

const W = 36, H = 24, D = 20; // width (x), height (y), depth (z)
```
Tank center at (0, H/2, 0). Floor at y=0.

Color schemes:
```
const PALETTES = [
  { body: 0xff7f2a, fin: 0xffb066 }, // orange
  { body: 0x2a7fff, fin: 0x6fb3ff }, // blue
  { body: 0xffd23f, fin: 0xff5533 }, // yellow-red
  { body: 0x9b5cff, fin: 0xc9a0ff }, // purple

  { body: 0x3ddc84, fin: 0x9fffc7 }, // green

createFish(x,y,z):
```
const pal = random palette
group = new THREE.Group()
body = Mesh(SphereGeometry(1,16,12), mat body)
body.scale.set(0.32, 0.5, 1.0)
body.castShadow = true
group.add(body)
// eyes
eyeL/R white sphere 0.11 at (±0.17, 0.12, 0.72)
pupil black 0.05 at (±0.19, 0.12, 0.8)
// tail pivot
tailPivot = Group at (0, 0, -0.95)
tail = Mesh(SphereGeometry(1,10,8), mat fin)
tail.scale.set(0.05, 0.55, 0.75)
tail.position.z = -0.35
tailPivot.add(tail)
group.add(tailPivot)
// dorsal fin
dorsal = Mesh(SphereGeometry(1,10,8), finMat)
dorsal.scale.set(0.04, 0.3, 0.55)
dorsal.position.set(0, 0.42, -0.05)
group.add(dorsal)
// pectoral fins
leftFin = Mesh(...fin) scale(0.04, 0.22, 0.28) at (0.28, -0.05, 0.35), rotation.z = -0.5
rightFin mirror
group.add both
// size
baseScale = 0.6 + Math.random()*0.6
group.scale.setScalar(baseScale)
group.position.set(x,y,z)
scene.add(group)
push to fishArray: {mesh: group, tail: tailPivot, leftFin, rightFin, velocity: random small Vector3, speed: 2.5 + rand*2, tailSpeed: 6+rand*4, phase: rand*PI*2, targetFood: null, avoidanceRadius: 2.5+rand*1.5, wanderTimer: 2+rand*3, yaw: rand*PI*2, pitch: 0, baseScale}
```

Initial 15 fish at random positions inside the tank.

Behavior update function updateFish(f, dt, t):
As above. Also handle targetFood existence: food object {mesh, pos..., eaten flag}. If f.targetFood.eaten → null.

Separation is O(n²) — fine for up to ~40 fish.

Wall steering:
```
const m = 2; // margin
if (p.x > W/2 - m) f.velocity.x -= (p.x - (W/2 - m)) * 2 * dt * 4;
```
Better: apply a steering acceleration:
```
let ax=0, ay=0, az=0;
if (p.x > W/2 - m) ax -= (p.x - (W/2-m)) * 3;
else if (p.x < -W/2 + m) ax += (-W/2 + m - p.x) * 3;
Same for z with D.
Vertical: if (p.y > H - 2) ay -= (p.y - (H-2))*3; if (p.y < 1.5) ay += (1.5 - p.y)*3;
f.velocity.x += ax*dt; etc.
```
And hard clamp position to bounds.

Food pursuit:
```
let food = null;
for (const fd of foodItems) {
  const d = f.mesh.position.distanceTo(fd.mesh.position);
  if (d < 15) { food = fd; break; }
}
f.targetFood = food;
if (food) {
  const dir = new THREE.Vector3().subVectors(food.mesh.position, f.mesh.position).normalize();
  f.velocity.lerp(dir.multiplyScalar(f.speed * 1.5), 0.06);
  if (f.mesh.position.distanceTo(food.mesh.position) < 1.8) {
    // eat
    scene.remove(food.mesh);
    foodItems.splice(indexOf, 1);
    f.baseScale = Math.min(f.baseScale * 1.05, 2.2);
    f.mesh.scale.setScalar(f.baseScale);
    spawnBubblesAt(food position, 5);
  }
}
```

Note: multiple fish may target the same food; once eaten, remove it; other fish's targets become stale — check `foodItems.includes(f.targetFood)` every frame.

Now food items: {mesh, vy, settled, settleTime}.

Food update:
```
for (i = foodItems.length-1; i>=0; i--) {
  const fd = foodItems[i];
  if (!fd.settled) {
    fd.vy = Math.max(fd.vy - 5*dt, -2.2);
    fd.mesh.position.y += fd.vy*dt;
    if (fd.mesh.position.y <= 0.35) { fd.settled = true; fd.settleTime = t; }
  } else {
    // slowly sink / fade; remove after 20 seconds
    if (t - fd.settleTime > 20) { scene.remove(fd.mesh); foodItems.splice(i,1); }
  }
}
```

Click → feed:
```
renderer.domElement.addEventListener('pointerdown', e => { downX=e.clientX; downY=e.clientY; });
renderer.domElement.addEventListener('pointerup', e => {
  if (Math.hypot(e.clientX-downX, e.clientY-downY) > 6) return;
  // compute point
  raycaster.setFromCamera({x: e.clientX/innerWidth*2-1, y: -(e.clientY/innerHeight)*2+1}, camera);
  camera.getWorldDirection(camDir);
  plane.normal.copy(camDir);
  plane.constant = -camDir.dot(TANK_CENTER);
  const pt = new THREE.Vector3();
  if (raycaster.ray.intersectPlane(plane, pt)) {
    pt.x = clamp(pt.x, -W/2+1.2, W/2-1.2);
    pt.y = clamp(pt.y, 1, H-1.2);
    pt.z = clamp(pt.z, -D/2+1.2, D/2-1.2);
    dropFood(pt);
  }
});
```

dropFood: create a small icosahedron/sphere 0.28, color 0x8b5a2b or pellet 0xffaa33, castShadow, push to foodItems with vy = -0.5.

Also spawn a few bubbles at the food location as feedback.

dorsal.position.set(0, 0.42, -0.05)
group.add(dorsal)
// pectoral fins
leftFin = Mesh(...fin) scale(0.04, 0.22, 0.28) at (0.28, -0.05, 0.35), rotation.z = -0.5
rightFin mirror
group.add both
// size
baseScale = 0.6 + Math.random()*0.6
group.scale.setScalar(baseScale)
group.position.set(x,y,z)
scene.add(group)
push to fishArray: {mesh: group, tail: tailPivot, leftFin, rightFin, velocity: random small Vector3, speed: 2.5 + rand*2, tailSpeed: 6+rand*4, phase: rand*PI*2, targetFood: null, avoidanceRadius: 2.5+rand*1.5, wanderTimer: 2+rand*3, yaw: rand*PI*2, pitch: 0, baseScale}
```

Initial 15 fish at random positions inside the tank.

Behavior update function updateFish(f, dt, t):
As above. Also handle targetFood existence: food object {mesh, pos..., eaten flag}. If f.targetFood.eaten → null.

Separation is O(n²) — fine for up to ~40 fish.

Wall steering:
```
const m = 2; // margin
if (p.x > W/2 - m) f.velocity.x -= (p.x - (W/2 - m)) * 2 * dt * 4;
```
Better: apply a steering acceleration:
```
let ax=0, ay=0, az=0;
if (p.x > W/2 - m) ax -= (p.x - (W/2-m)) * 3;
else if (p.x < -W/2 + m) ax += (-W/2 + m - p.x) * 3;
Same for z with D.
Vertical: if (p.y > H - 2) ay -= (p.y - (H-2))*3; if (p.y < 1.5) ay += (1.5 - p.y)*3;
f.velocity.x += ax*dt; etc.
```
And hard clamp position to bounds.

Food pursuit:
```
let food = null;
for (const fd of foodItems) {
  const d = f.mesh.position.distanceTo(fd.mesh.position);
  if (d < 15) { food = fd; break; }
}
f.targetFood = food;
if (food) {
  const dir = new THREE.Vector3().subVectors(food.mesh.position, f.mesh.position).normalize();
  f.velocity.lerp(dir.multiplyScalar(f.speed * 1.5), 0.06);
  if (f.mesh.position.distanceTo(food.mesh.position) < 1.8) {
    // eat
    scene.remove(food.mesh);
    foodItems.splice(indexOf, 1);
    f.baseScale = Math.min(f.baseScale * 1.05, 2.2);
    f.mesh.scale.setScalar(f.baseScale);
    spawnBubblesAt(food position, 5);
  }
}
```

Note: multiple fish may target the same food; once eaten, remove it; other fish's targets become stale — check `foodItems.includes(f.targetFood)` every frame.

Now food items: {mesh, vy, settled, settleTime}.

Food update:
```
for (i = foodItems.length-1; i>=0; i--) {
  const fd = foodItems[i];
  if (!fd.settled) {
    fd.vy = Math.max(fd.vy - 5*dt, -2.2);
    fd.mesh.position.y += fd.vy*dt;
    if (fd.mesh.position.y <= 0.35) { fd.settled = true; fd.settleTime = t; }
  } else {
    // slowly sink / fade; remove after 20 seconds
    if (t - fd.settleTime > 20) { scene.remove(fd.mesh); foodItems.splice(i,1); }
  }
}
```

Click → feed:
```
renderer.domElement.addEventListener('pointerdown', e => { downX=e.clientX; downY=e.clientY; });
renderer.domElement.addEventListener('pointerup', e => {
  if (Math.hypot(e.clientX-downX, e.clientY-downY) > 6) return;
  // compute point
  raycaster.setFromCamera({x: e.clientX/innerWidth*2-1, y: -(e.clientY/innerHeight)*2+1}, camera);
  camera.getWorldDirection(camDir);
  plane.normal.copy(camDir);
  plane.constant = -camDir.dot(TANK_CENTER);
  const pt = new THREE.Vector3();
  if (raycaster.ray.intersectPlane(plane, pt)) {
    pt.x = clamp(pt.x, -W/2+1.2, W/2-1.2);
    pt.y = clamp(pt.y, 1, H-1.2);
    pt.z = clamp(pt.z, -D/2+1.2, D/2-1.2);
    dropFood(pt);
  }
});
```

Now the main JS. Let me write it carefully.

```
// ==== Setup ====
const W = 36, H = 24, D = 20;
const TANK_CENTER = new THREE.Vector3(0, H/2, 0);

const scene = new THREE.Scene();
scene.fog = new THREE.FogExp2(0x0a2f5c, 0.011);

const camera = new THREE.PerspectiveCamera(55, innerWidth/innerHeight, 0.1, 300);
camera.position.set(26, 22, 34);

const renderer = new THREE.WebGLRenderer({ antialias: true, alpha: true });
renderer.setSize(innerWidth, innerHeight);
renderer.setPixelRatio(Math.min(devicePixelRatio, 2));
renderer.shadowMap.enabled = true;
renderer.shadowMap.type = THREE.PCFSoftShadowMap;
document.body.appendChild(renderer.domElement);

const controls = new THREE.OrbitControls(camera, renderer.domElement);
controls.target.copy(TANK_CENTER);
controls.enableDamping = true;
controls.dampingFactor = 0.06;
controls.minDistance = 10;
controls.maxDistance = 60;
controls.maxPolarAngle = Math.PI / 1.8;
```

Lighting:
```
const ambient = new THREE.AmbientLight(0x404040, 0.4);
scene.add(ambient);

const sun = new THREE.DirectionalLight(0xfff1d6, 1.15);
sun.position.set(24, 40, 18);
sun.castShadow = true;
sun.shadow.mapSize.set(2048, 2048);
sun.shadow.camera.near = 1;
sun.shadow.camera.far = 120;
sun.shadow.camera.left = -32;
sun.shadow.camera.right = 32;
sun.shadow.camera.top = 32;
sun.shadow.camera.bottom = -32;
sun.shadow.bias = -0.0004;
scene.add(sun);

const pl1 = new THREE.PointLight(0x1fd0ff, 0.9, 70, 2);
pl1.position.set(-14, 17, -7);
scene.add(pl1);
const pl2 = new THREE.PointLight(0x3a6bff, 0.9, 70, 2);
pl2.position.set(14, 9, 9);
scene.add(pl2);
```

Tank:
```
function buildTank() {
  const g = new THREE.BoxGeometry(W, H, D);
  const glass = new THREE.MeshPhysicalMaterial({
    color: 0xbfe6ff, transmission: 0.95, opacity: 0.16, transparent: true,
    roughness: 0.06, metalness: 0, side: THREE.DoubleSide, depthWrite: false
  });
  const box = new THREE.Mesh(g, glass);
  box.position.copy(TANK_CENTER);
  scene.add(box);

  const edges = new THREE.LineSegments(
    new THREE.EdgesGeometry(g),
    new THREE.LineBasicMaterial({ color: 0x8fd4ff, transparent: true, opacity: 0.55 })
  );
  edges.position.copy(TANK_CENTER);
  scene.add(edges);
}
```

Sand:
```
function buildSand() {
  const geo = new THREE.PlaneGeometry(W, D, 40, 26);
  geo.rotateX(-Math.PI/2);
  const pos = geo.attributes.position;
  for (let i=0;i<pos.count;i++){
    pos.setY(i, pos.getY(i) + (Math.random()*0.28 - 0.02));
  }
  geo.computeVertexNormals();
  const mat = new THREE.MeshStandardMaterial({ color: 0xd8bd85, roughness: 1.0, metalness: 0.0 });
  const m = new THREE.Mesh(geo, mat);
  m.receiveShadow = true;
  scene.add(m);
}
```

Rocks:
```
function buildRocks() {
  for (let i=0;i<8;i++){
    const r = 0.9 + Math.random()*1.1;
    const geo = new THREE.DodecahedronGeometry(r, 0);
    const p = geo.attributes.position;
    for (let j=0;j<p.count;j++){
      p.setXYZ(j, p.getX(j)*(1+(Math.random()-0.5)*0.4), p.getY(j)*(1+(Math.random()-0.5)*0.4), p.getZ(j)*(1+(Math.random()-0.5)*0.4));
    }
    geo.computeVertexNormals();
    const mat = new THREE.MeshStandardMaterial({ color: new THREE.Color(0x6b7280).offsetHSL(0, 0, (Math.random()-0.5)*0.12), roughness: 0.95 });
    const rock = new THREE.Mesh(geo, mat);
    rock.position.set((Math.random()-0.5)*(W-6), r*0.55, (Math.random()-0.5)*(D-6));
    rock.rotation.set(Math.random()*Math.PI, Math.random()*Math.PI, Math.random()*Math.PI);
    rock.castShadow = true;
    rock.receiveShadow = true;
    scene.add(rock);
  }
}
```

Seaweed:
```
const weeds = [];
function buildWeeds() {
  const greens = [0x2fae5f, 0x39c46d, 0x1f8f4e, 0x54d97a];
  for (let i=0;i<12;i++){
    const clump = new THREE.Group();
    const n = 2 + Math.floor(Math.random()*3);
    for (let b=0;b<n;b++){
      const h = 2.5 + Math.random()*3.5;
      const bx = (Math.random()-0.5)*1.2;
      const bz = (Math.random()-0.5)*1.2;
      const pts = [];
      const lean = (Math.random()-0.5)*1.6;
      for (let s=0;s<=4;s++){
        const t = s/4;
        pts.push(new THREE.Vector3(
          bx + lean*t*t + Math.sin(t*3+b)*0.15,
          t*h,
          bz + Math.cos(t*2.5+b)*0.15
        ));
      }
      const curve = new THREE.CatmullRomCurve3(pts);
      const tube = new THREE.TubeGeometry(curve, 16, 0.14*(0.8+Math.random()*0.5), 5, false);
      const mat = new THREE.MeshStandardMaterial({ color: greens[Math.floor(Math.random()*greens.length)], roughness: 0.7, side: THREE.DoubleSide });
      const blade = new THREE.Mesh(tube, mat);
      blade.castShadow = true;
      clump.add(blade);
    }
    clump.position.set((Math.random()-0.5)*(W-5), 0.1, (Math.random()-0.5)*(D-5));
    scene.add(clump);
    weeds.push({ obj: clump, phase: Math.random()*Math.PI*2 });
  }
}
```

Fish:
```
const fishArray = [];
const PALETTES = [
  { body: 0xff7f2a, fin: 0xffb066 },
  { body: 0x2a7fff, fin: 0x6fb3ff },
  { body: 0xffd23f, fin: 0xff5533 },
  { body: 0x9b5cff, fin: 0xc9a0ff },
  { body: 0xff3b3b, fin: 0xff8a8a },
  { body: 0x3ddc84, fin: 0x9fffc7 },
  { body: 0xff7ab8, fin: 0xffc2dd },
  { body: 0xffc93c, fin: 0xffe08a },
];

function createFish(x, y, z) {
  const pal = PALETTES[Math.floor(Math.random()*PALETTES.length)];
  const bodyMat = new THREE.MeshStandardMaterial({ color: pal.body, roughness: 0.45, metalness: 0.15 });
  const finMat = new THREE.MeshStandardMaterial({ color: pal.fin, roughness: 0.5, metalness: 0.1, transparent: true, opacity: 0.92 });

  const group = new THREE.Group();

  // body
  const body = new THREE.Mesh(new THREE.SphereGeometry(1, 18, 14), bodyMat);
  body.scale.set(0.34, 0.5, 1.0);
  body.castShadow = true;
  group.add(body);

  // head highlight (slightly lighter snout)
  const snout = new THREE.Mesh(new THREE.SphereGeometry(0.32, 12, 10), bodyMat);
  snout.scale.set(0.8, 0.85, 1.15);
  snout.position.set(0, 0.02, 0.72);
  group.add(snout);

  // eyes
  const eyeWhite = new THREE.MeshStandardMaterial({ color: 0xf4f8ff, roughness: 0.25, metalness: 0.1 });
  const pupilMat = new THREE.MeshStandardMaterial({ color: 0x0a0d12, roughness: 0.3, metalness: 0.2 });
  [-1, 1].forEach(s => {
    const eye = new THREE.Mesh(new THREE.SphereGeometry(0.115, 12, 10), eyeWhite);
    eye.position.set(0.17*s, 0.13, 0.7);
    group.add(eye);
    const pupil = new THREE.Mesh(new THREE.SphereGeometry(0.055, 10, 8), pupilMat);
    pupil.position.set(0.19*s, 0.13, 0.79);
    group.add(pupil);
  });

  // tail (pivot at rear of body)
  const tailPivot = new THREE.Group();
  tailPivot.position.set(0, 0, -0.92);
  const tail = new THREE.Mesh(new THREE.SphereGeometry(1, 12, 10), finMat);
  tail.scale.set(0.05, 0.55, 0.72);
  tail.position.z = -0.34;
  tail.castShadow = true;
  tailPivot.add(tail);
  group.add(tailPivot);

  // dorsal fin
  const dorsal = new THREE.Mesh(new THREE.SphereGeometry(1, 10, 8), finMat);
  dorsal.scale.set(0.045, 0.3, 0.55);
  dorsal.position.set(0, 0.42, -0.05);
  dorsal.castShadow = true;
  group.add(dorsal);

  // pectoral fins
  const leftFin = new THREE.Mesh(new THREE.SphereGeometry(1, 10, 8), finMat);
  leftFin.scale.set(0.04, 0.22, 0.3);
  leftFin.position.set(0.28, -0.06, 0.32);
  leftFin.rotation.z = -0.55;
  group.add(leftFin);
  const rightFin = new THREE.Mesh(new THREE.SphereGeometry(1, 10, 8), finMat);
  rightFin.scale.set(0.04, 0.22, 0.3);
  rightFin.position.set(-0.28, -0.06, 0.32);
  rightFin.rotation.z = 0.55;
  group.add(rightFin);

  const baseScale = 0.6 + Math.random()*0.6;
  group.scale.setScalar(baseScale);
  group.position.set(x, y, z);
  scene.add(group);

  const v = new THREE.Vector3((Math.random()-0.5)*2, (Math.random()-0.5)*0.6, (Math.random()-0.5)*2);
  fishArray.push({
    mesh: group,
    tail: tailPivot,
    leftFin, rightFin,
    velocity: v,
    speed: 2.4 + Math.random()*2.2,
    tailSpeed: 6 + Math.random()*4,
    phase: Math.random()*Math.PI*2,
    targetFood: null,
    avoidanceRadius: 2.6 + Math.random()*1.6,
    wanderTimer: 2 + Math.random()*3,
    yaw: Math.random()*Math.PI*2,
    pitch: 0,
    baseScale
  });
}
```

Wait — the fish's orientation. I'm building the fish facing +Z (head/snout at +z, tail at -z). So to orient it toward its velocity, I need yaw = atan2(v.x, v.z). Because when yaw=0, the fish faces +Z. Rotating the group by yaw around Y makes it face direction (sin(yaw), 0, cos(yaw)). The normalized horizontal velocity direction is (v.x, v.z)/len. So target yaw = Math.atan2(v.x, v.z). Good.

Pitch: I want the nose to dip when descending. rotation.x positive pitches the nose down? For an object facing +Z, a positive rotation around X rotates +Z toward -Y (nose down). Actually, rotation around X: positive angle rotates the +Z axis toward the -Y axis? Let me recall the right-hand rule: rotation around +X, positive angle takes +Y toward +Z, and +Z toward -Y. So the nose (+Z) goes toward -Y (down) with positive rotation.x. So when moving down (vy<0), we want the nose down → positive pitch. pitch = clamp(atan2(-vy, horizLen), ...) hmm.

Let me define: pitch = clamp( Math.atan2( -v.y, horizSpeed ) * 0.6, -0.5, 0.5 ). If v.y is negative (descending), -v.y is positive, atan2 gives a positive value → nose down. Good. If ascending, nose up (negative pitch). Good.

Smoothly: f.pitch = THREE.MathUtils.lerp(f.pitch, targetPitch, 0.06). And f.mesh.rotation.x = f.pitch.

Yaw smoothing with angle wrap:
```
function shortestAngle(a){ a = a % (Math.PI*2); if (a > Math.PI) a -= Math.PI*2; if (a < -Math.PI) a += Math.PI*2; return a; }
f.yaw += shortestAngle(targetYaw - f.yaw) * 0.09;
f.mesh.rotation.y = f.yaw;
```

Good.

Now the full updateFish:

```
const _dir = new THREE.Vector3();
function updateFish(f, dt, t) {
  const p = f.mesh.position;
  const v = f.velocity;

  // --- Food pursuit / eating ---
  let food = null;
  if (f.targetFood && foodItems.indexOf(f.targetFood) !== -1) food = f.targetFood;
  else {
    for (let i=0;i<foodItems.length;i++){
      if (p.distanceTo(foodItems[i].mesh.position) < 15) { food = foodItems[i]; break; }
    }
  }
  f.targetFood = food;

  if (food) {
    _dir.subVectors(food.mesh.position, p);
    const dist = _dir.length();
    if (dist > 0.001) _dir.normalize();
    _dir.multiplyScalar(f.speed * 1.5);
    v.lerp(_dir, 0.07);
    if (dist < 1.9) {
      // eat!
      scene.remove(food.mesh);
      foodItems.splice(foodItems.indexOf(food), 1);
      f.targetFood = null;
      f.baseScale = Math.min(f.baseScale * 1.05, 2.3);
      f.mesh.scale.setScalar(f.baseScale);
      spawnBubblesAt(food.mesh.position, 6);
    }
  }

  // --- Separation (avoid collisions) ---
  for (let i=0;i<fishArray.length;i++){
    const o = fishArray[i];
    if (o === f) continue;
    const dx = p.x - o.mesh.position.x, dy = p.y - o.mesh.position.y, dz = p.z - o.mesh.position.z;
    const d2 = dx*dx + dy*dy + dz*dz;
    const rr = f.avoidanceRadius;
    if (d2 < rr*rr && d2 > 0.0001){
      const d = Math.sqrt(d2);
      const push = (rr - d) / rr; // 0..1
      v.x += (dx/d) * push * 3.2 * dt * 10;
      v.y += (dy/d) * push * 3.2 * dt * 10;
      v.z += (dz/d) * push * 3.2 * dt * 10;
    }
  }
```

Hmm, the scaling of the separation force. Let me simplify: add a proportional impulse. Let me do this: strength = push * 14 * dt. Then v += dir_normalized * strength. With push up to 1 and dt~0.016, strength up to 0.22 per frame → accumulates over several frames. Reasonable. Let me tune: `const str = push * 16 * dt;` and add `v.x += (dx/d)*str;` etc.

Continuing:
```
  // --- Wall reflection (steering) ---
  const mx = W/2 - 1.6, mz = D/2 - 1.6, mTop = H - 1.6, mBot = 1.2;
  let ax=0, ay=0, az=0;
  if (p.x > mx) ax -= (p.x - mx) * 4.5;
  else if (p.x < -mx) ax += (-mx - p.x) * 4.5;
  if (p.z > mz) az -= (p.z - mz) * 4.5;
  else if (p.z < -mz) az += (-mz - p.z) * 4.5;
  if (p.y > mTop) ay -= (p.y - mTop) * 4.5;
  else if (p.y < mBot) ay += (mBot - p.y) * 4.5;
  v.x += ax*dt; v.y += ay*dt; v.z += az*dt;

  // --- Random wandering ---
  f.wanderTimer -= dt;
  if (f.wanderTimer <= 0){
    f.wanderTimer = 2 + Math.random()*3.5;
    if (!food){
      v.x += (Math.random()-0.5) * 2.2;
      v.y += (Math.random()-0.5) * 0.9;
      v.z += (Math.random()-0.5) * 2.2;
    }
  }

  // --- Speed regulation ---
  const len = v.length();
  if (len > 0.0001){
    const target = food ? f.speed*1.5 : f.speed;
    const nl = THREE.MathUtils.lerp(len, target, 0.045);
    v.multiplyScalar(nl / len);
  }

  // --- Integration ---
  p.addScaledVector(v, dt);
  // hard clamp
  p.x = THREE.MathUtils.clamp(p.x, -W/2+0.8, W/2-0.8);
  p.y = THREE.MathUtils.clamp(p.y, 0.9, H-0.9);
  p.z = THREE.MathUtils.clamp(p.z, -D/2+0.8, D/2-0.8);

  // --- Orientation ---
  const hv = Math.sqrt(v.x*v.x + v.z*v.z);
  const targetYaw = Math.atan2(v.x, v.z);
  f.yaw += shortestAngle(targetYaw - f.yaw) * 0.09;
  f.mesh.rotation.y = f.yaw;
  const targetPitch = hv > 0.01 ? THREE.MathUtils.clamp(Math.atan2(-v.y, hv) * 0.55, -0.55, 0.55) : 0;
  f.pitch = THREE.MathUtils.lerp(f.pitch, targetPitch, 0.06);
  f.mesh.rotation.x = f.pitch;

  // --- Fin/tail animation ---
  f.tail.rotation.y = Math.sin(t * f.tailSpeed + f.phase) * 0.6;
  f.leftFin.rotation.z  = -0.55 + Math.sin(t * 4 + f.phase) * 0.22;
  f.rightFin.rotation.z =  0.55 + Math.sin(t * 4 + f.phase + 1) * 0.22;
}
```

Wait, there's an issue: when the fish eats, it uses `food.mesh.position` after `scene.remove(food.mesh)` — the position is still valid. Good.

But note: after eating, we set f.targetFood=null, but the loop continues; food is still defined. That's fine.

Also note: `spawnBubblesAt` — let me define it to create a few short-lived bubbles at a given position. Reuse the bubble system.

Bubbles:
```
const bubbles = [];
function makeBubble(x, y, z, lifeBoost) {
  const r = 0.12 + Math.random()*0.3;
  const mat = new THREE.MeshPhysicalMaterial({
    color: 0xcfeeff, transmission: 0.7, opacity: 0.35, transparent: true,
    roughness: 0.15, metalness: 0, clearcoat: 0.6
  });
  const m = new THREE.Mesh(new THREE.SphereGeometry(r, 12, 10), mat);
  m.position.set(x, y, z);
  scene.add(m);
  bubbles.push({
    mesh: m,
    speed: 1.6 + Math.random()*2.4,
    phase: Math.random()*Math.PI*2,
    baseX: x, baseZ: z
  });
}
function initBubbles(n){ for(let i=0;i<n;i++) makeBubble((Math.random()-0.5)*(W-4), 0.5+Math.random()*(H-2), (Math.random()-0.5)*(D-4)); }
function spawnBubblesAt(pos, n){ for(let i=0;i<n;i++) makeBubble(pos.x+(Math.random()-0.5)*1.5, pos.y+(Math.random()-0.5)*1.5, pos.z+(Math.random()-0.5)*1.5); }

function updateBubbles(dt, t){
  for (let i=bubbles.length-1;i>=0;i--){
    const b = bubbles[i];
    b.mesh.position.y += b.speed * dt;
    b.mesh.position.x = b.baseX + Math.sin(t*1.4 + b.phase)*0.5;
    b.mesh.position.z = b.baseZ + Math.cos(t*1.1 + b.phase)*0.5;
    if (b.mesh.position.y > H - 0.6){
      // reset to the bottom
      b.baseX = (Math.random()-0.5)*(W-4);
      b.baseZ = (Math.random()-0.5)*(D-4);
      b.mesh.position.set(b.baseX, 0.4, b.baseZ);
    }
  }
}
```

Hmm, but the bubble's x/z are driven by sin around baseX — meaning they sway. Good. But if a bubble is spawned near the top (from food at high y), it rises a bit and resets to the bottom. Fine.

But there's a subtle bug: I set `b.mesh.position.x = b.baseX + sin(...)`, but baseX is the original x; if I want to allow drift, that's fine. OK.

Memory: bubbles are infinite (reset, not removed), so count stays constant unless we add more. Good — no leak.

Food:
```
const foodItems = [];
function dropFood(pos){
  const mat = new THREE.MeshStandardMaterial({ color: 0xff9a3c, roughness: 0.6, emissive: 0x331500 });
  const m = new THREE.Mesh(new THREE.IcosahedronGeometry(0.32, 0), mat);
  m.position.copy(pos);
  m.castShadow = true;
  scene.add(m);
  foodItems.push({ mesh: m, vy: -0.4, settled: false, settleTime: 0 });
  spawnBubblesAt(pos, 4);
}
function updateFood(dt, t){
  for (let i=foodItems.length-1;i>=0;i--){
    const fd = foodItems[i];
    if (!fd.settled){
      fd.vy = Math.max(fd.vy - 4.5*dt, -2.0);
      fd.mesh.position.y += fd.vy * dt;
      if (fd.mesh.position.y <= 0.4){
        fd.mesh.position.y = 0.4;
        fd.settled = true;
        fd.settleTime = t;
      }
    } else {
      // slowly "dissolve": shrink and remove after a while
      const age = t - fd.settleTime;
      if (age > 14){
        scene.remove(fd.mesh);
        foodItems.splice(i,1);
      }
    }
  }
}
```

Good.

Now the main loop:
```
const clock = new THREE.Clock();
let frames = 0, fpsTime = 0, fps = 60;

function animate(){
  requestAnimationFrame(animate);
  const dt = Math.min(clock.getDelta(), 0.05);
  const t = clock.elapsedTime;

  // fish
  for (let i=0;i<fishArray.length;i++) updateFish(fishArray[i], dt, t);
  // bubbles
  updateBubbles(dt, t);
  // food
  updateFood(dt, t);
  // seaweed sway
  for (let i=0;i<weeds.length;i++){
    const w = weeds[i];
    w.obj.rotation.x = Math.sin(t*0.9 + w.phase) * 0.07;
    w.obj.rotation.z = Math.cos(t*0.7 + w.phase) * 0.07;
  }
  // subtle water light flicker
  pl1.intensity = 0.85 + Math.sin(t*2.1)*0.12;
  pl2.intensity = 0.85 + Math.cos(t*1.7)*0.12;

  controls.update();
  renderer.render(scene, camera);

  // FPS
  frames++;
  fpsTime += dt;
  if (fpsTime >= 0.5){
    fps = Math.round(frames / fpsTime);
    frames = 0; fpsTime = 0;
    document.getElementById('fpsVal').textContent = fps;
  }
}
```

```
const raycaster = new THREE.Raycaster();
const feedPlane = new THREE.Plane();
const _camDir = new THREE.Vector3();
const _hit = new THREE.Vector3();
let downX=0, downY=0;

renderer.domElement.addEventListener('pointerdown', e => { downX=e.clientX; downY=e.clientY; });
renderer.domElement.addEventListener('pointerup', e => {
  if (Math.abs(e.clientX-downX) + Math.abs(e.clientY-downY) > 7) return;
  const ndc = new THREE.Vector2((e.clientX/innerWidth)*2-1, -(e.clientY/innerHeight)*2+1);
  raycaster.setFromCamera(ndc, camera);
  camera.getWorldDirection(_camDir);
  feedPlane.normal.copy(_camDir);
  feedPlane.constant = -_camDir.dot(TANK_CENTER);
  if (raycaster.ray.intersectPlane(feedPlane, _hit)){
    _hit.x = THREE.MathUtils.clamp(_hit.x, -W/2+1.3, W/2-1.3);
    _hit.y = THREE.MathUtils.clamp(_hit.y, 1.2, H-1.3);
    _hit.z = THREE.MathUtils.clamp(_hit.z, -D/2+1.3, D/2-1.3);
    dropFood(_hit);
  }
});

4. `clock.elapsedTime` — Clock has an elapsedTime property that updates on getDelta/getElapsedTime. Actually, `getDelta()` updates `this.elapsedTime`. Let me confirm: THREE.Clock: `getDelta()` computes and returns delta, and sets `this.elapsedTime = this.elapsedTime + delta`? Let me recall the source:

```
getDelta: function () {
  ...
  var diff = ...;
  this.oldTime = this.time;
  this.time = this.time + diff;
  this.elapsedTime = this.time; // hmm
  return diff;
}
```
Actually, the real Clock source:
```
start(){ this.startTime = now(); this.oldTime = this.startTime; this.elapsedTime = 0; this.running = true; }
getElapsedTime(){ this.getDelta(); return this.elapsedTime; }
getDelta(){
  var diff = 0;
  if (this.autoStart && !this.running){ this.start(); }
  if (this.running){
    var newTime = now();
    diff = (newTime - this.oldTime) / 1000;
    this.oldTime = newTime;
    this.elapsedTime += diff;
  }
  return diff;
}
```
So after calling getDelta(), `clock.elapsedTime` is the total elapsed time. Good — using `clock.elapsedTime` after getDelta is valid.

Now let me verify the fish orientation sign: when moving in +X (v.x>0, v.z≈0), targetYaw = atan2(v.x, v.z) = atan2(+, 0) = +PI/2. Rotating the group by +PI/2 around Y: the +Z axis (nose) rotates to... rotating +Z by +90° around +Y: +Z → +X? Rotation around Y by θ maps (x,z): x' = x cosθ + z sinθ, z' = -x sinθ + z cosθ. Nose initially at (0,0,1). After θ=+90°: x' = 0*cos + 1*sin = 1, z' = -0*sin + 1*cos = 0. So the nose points to +X. Correct! Matches velocity +X.

When moving in -Z (v.z<0): targetYaw = atan2(0, -1) = PI. Nose (0,0,1) rotated by PI around Y: x'=0, z' = 1*cos(PI) = -1 → nose to -Z. Correct.
